# PHASE 2 — PER-FORMULA LEDGER

**Status** FIRST PASS. **8.7 % of the required determinations are made; 91.3 % are UNVERIFIED.**
Nothing in this file marks a Phase-2 requirement COMPLETE.

P02-107 requires a verdict per formula and P02-108-111 fix the vocabulary to
**PASS / FAIL / WARNING / UNVERIFIED**. P02-112 forbids *"probably correct"*. This ledger
therefore assigns a verdict ONLY where evidence already held determines one, and UNVERIFIED
with a stated reason everywhere else. **UNVERIFIED here means not yet examined, not benign.**

**Scope** the **1,288 retained engineering relations** (register class C1) x the **20 questions**
of P02-087-106 = **25,760 determinations** required.

**Tooling** `scratchpad/reg_parse.mjs` + `build_p2_ledger.mjs` + `emit_ledger.mjs`. Neither runs
the engine nor inspects any formula's numerical output, so no part of this is self-certifying.
The register is parsed by taking the columns before `equation as coded` from the left and those
after it from the right, because equations legitimately contain `||` and a naive split is corrupt
(N-21). The parse is validated against the controlled vocabularies of the trailing columns:
`confidence` resolves to exactly 3 values, `impl` to 1, `unitVerif` to 5, and the `unitVerif`
split reproduces the register's independently recorded 165/198/135/6/784 exactly.

## 2. What the sixteen answered questions rest on

- **Q3 units (P02-089)** — PASS where the register resolved the units and dimensional
  propagation found them consistent (DECLARED or DERIVED). **FAIL on the 6 rows carrying a
  recorded dimensional CONFLICT.** UNVERIFIED for AMBIGUOUS and UNRESOLVED, which is the
  **28.2 % units-resolution cap**; its only lever is source-side naming, a production change
  that is not authorised (N-18).
- **Q9 duplication (P02-095)** — fully determined, the only question that is. Decided by exact
  equation-shape match inside the retained set. Note this asks only about duplication WITHIN
  the retained relations; the register's 422 C7 sites are duplicate implementations that were
  excluded from C1 by the classification rule, and are therefore not re-counted here.
- **Q10 coefficient sourcing (P02-096)** — PASS on DIRECTLY STATED and MEASURED FROM SOURCE.
  **WARNING on the 25 rows declared an ENGINEERING ASSUMPTION**: declared is not sourced.
  UNVERIFIED on the **944 rows (73.3 %) whose source is UNVERIFIED** — this figure was carried
  forward unverified in N-21 and is **now re-derived and confirmed** at 944/1288.
- **Q20 zero/negative/NaN (P02-106)** — **ASYMMETRIC BY DESIGN, and it yields no PASS.** The
  absence of any guard near a site is evidence the case is unhandled, so it is recorded as a
  **WARNING (253 relations)**. The presence of a guard does NOT show that it covers this
  relation, so it yields UNVERIFIED rather than PASS. A mechanical scan cannot earn a PASS here.
  **Three of the 253 are hand-verified rather than scanned** (`gearlayout.js` l.173, 176, 181):
  the zero-denominator fallback there does catch 0, but NaN is falsy, so a NaN *numerator* passes
  straight through to the output. Mutation returns NaN, and an undefined nose-wheel count returns
  a plausible-looking 1,137.2 kg against an 81.6 kg baseline. A guard that is present and does not
  cover the case is the WARNING condition, not the UNVERIFIED one. Latent, not live:
  `landinggear.js` always supplies the wheel counts.

- **Q1, Q4, Q5 and Q11 — hand-audited, not scanned, and only where the evidence is in hand.** The
  first determinations on these four questions come from the `src/engine/gearlayout.js` audit:
  **19 relations, 48 determinations**, each traceable to a stated basis in its row's notes and to a
  probe kept in the session scratchpad. Nothing here is mechanical, and nothing outside that one
  file is claimed. **Q10 was deliberately not touched**: the gate binds it to the register's
  `source` column with no escape clause, the register records those rows UNVERIFIED, and editing
  the register so that it agrees with a conclusion drawn from it is the F-42 error.
  - **Q1 mathematical expression (P02-087)** — **19 PASS**, earned three ways: exhaustive
    enumeration over a closed domain (the tyre table, 11 of 11 designations parse to the correct
    section width), algebra (the moment balance `x_main = (x_cg - f x_nose) / (1 - f)` inverted
    correctly), and an exact identity (the mass partition reproduces `landingGear().mass` to
    1.9e-16 on all six configurations).
  - **Q4 sign convention (P02-090)** — **17 PASS**: x measured aft from the nose and z positive up,
    with `x_nose < x_cg < x_main` and static clearance > 0 on all six configurations. The two
    tyre-width relations are **left UNVERIFIED on purpose** — a section width is a positive
    magnitude carrying no sign convention, and a vacuous PASS is still an unearned determination.
  - **Q5 reference conditions (P02-091)** — **1 WARNING**. The vertical criterion requires static
    clearance to cover the fitted strut stroke, but `landinggear.js`'s energy balance treats tyre
    deflection as a **separate co-absorber**, so at full compression the aircraft descends by the
    stroke *plus* the tyre deflection. WARNING and not FAIL, because that term is never the binding
    one: the strut-length term binds on all six configurations with 0.18-0.23 m of margin, and no
    configuration strikes the ground. **An incomplete criterion is not a failure.**
  - **Q11 hidden double-counting (P02-097)** — **8 PASS**, on the mass relations only, where the
    partition identity is the evidence. The geometric relations are left UNVERIFIED because the
    question does not bite on them and nothing measured here would settle it.

## 3. The two register columns that already state Phase 2 is undone

Independent of anything measured here, the register itself records for **all 1,288** relations:

- `validation` = **"NOT VALIDATED — Phase 2"**
- `indep verif` = **"NO — no independent calculation"**

So Phase 2 has no independently recomputed relation anywhere in the engine. That is consistent
with 0 of 112 requirements COMPLETE and with the no-self-certification rule: an independent
calculation is exactly what has not been done.

RP-1060 p.343 states that at maximum CL/CD the induced drag coefficient EQUALS the zero-lift drag coefficient, so k CL^2 = CD0, CL* = sqrt(CD0/k) and L/D = CL*/(2 CD0) = 0.5/sqrt(CD0 k). Reproduced at relative 0.0e+0 over 64 polars, and against a numerical maximisation of CL/CD on a 2e-4 grid at 1.9e-8, which is the grid's own resolution; 2: CD0 is the zero-lift drag coefficient and k the induced-drag factor 1/(pi AR e), the primary's own symbols; 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 17: a pure function of the polar coefficients with no fixed point; 19: measured homogeneous of degree -1/2 in CD0 and +1/2 in AR, both EXACT on doubling; 20: WARNING, hand-verified. polar() guards nothing: CD0 = 0 returns Infinity for both LDmax and climbParamMax, a negative CD0 returns NaN, and AR = 0 or e = 0 returns 0, each resolved and unflagged, measured by execution. Independently, the exact closed form is (3 pi)^(3/4)/4 = 1.34475558, so the primary's printed 1.345 carries a rounding of 1.818e-4 and the module inherits exactly that - its climbParamMax differs from a numerical maximisation of CL^1.5/CD over its own polar by 1.818e-4 and by nothing else; 2: A is aspect ratio, e the airplane efficiency factor and CD,o the zero-lift drag coefficient, the primary's own symbols; 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 17: a pure function of the polar coefficients; 19: measured homogeneous of degree +3/4 in A e and -1/4 in CD0, both EXACT on doubling, which is the exponent pair the primary prints; 20: WARNING, hand-verified. polar() guards nothing: CD0 = 0 returns Infinity for both LDmax and climbParamMax, a negative CD0 returns NaN, and AR = 0 or e = 0 returns 0, each resolved and unflagged, measured by execution. The 'hi' values of the four sections with a low-turbulence-tunnel reading are 0.0055, 0.0068, 0.0042 and 0.0038 against 0.0062, 0.0068, 0.0045 and 0.0044 (TR-824 charts, R 6 to 8.8 million): equal on one and 7 to 14 % low on three; 8: not extrapolated: clamped to the 'lo' value below Re 1e6 and to the 'hi' value above 7e6, measured; 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 10: source DIRECTLY STATED; 17: a pure function of the section record and the Reynolds number; 19: linear in Reynolds number between the anchors, measured; 20: WARNING, hand-verified by execution: a NaN Reynolds number returns NaN; a negative one returns the 'lo' value and an infinite one the 'hi' value, both without notice |

A helicopter-population coefficient on disc area is carried to disc loadings of 12 to 13 lb/ft^2. NDARC's alternative weight-based typical-hub scaling, 1.4 ((W/N)/1000)^(2/3) ft^2, equals the disc-area value only near 3.45 lb/ft^2 and is 3.79, 5.66 and 4.24 times it on the lift+cruise, hybrid and hybrid-pusher; re-sized at those values MTOW rises 69.5, 38.8 and 48.5 %, and at NDARC's low-drag weight scaling 19.7, 15.8 and 16.5 %. | FORMULA-AERO-197 | `src/engine/wingPosition.js:130` | P | P | U | P | U | U | U | U | P | U | U | U | U | U | U | U | P | P | W | W | 1: the textbook secant update x2 = x1 - g1 (x1 - x0) / (g1 - g0), clamped to the station range; the same form converges on a test cubic to a residual of 4.8e-16. On the UI default path, four winged layouts and five targets (0.05 to 0.30), the solver converged on all 20 runs in 3 to 5 full sizings, each to the single root found independently from a 119-station scan of the un-wrapped sizing with bisection, and within its 1e-3 tolerance on every run; 2: the next station at which the full sizing is evaluated; 4: sign-correct: the update moves toward the root on all 20 runs; 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 17: not circular, as AERO-196: a root find on a function of one variable; 18: guarded against its failure modes by source inspection - equal function values break before the division, a non-finite or sub-1e-6 step breaks, a revisited station hands over to a bracketed bisection, and two independent ceilings (45 sizings, 60 iterations) bound it - and measured stable on all 20 runs; 19: WARNING, measured: the function the secant sees is QUANTISED - the sizing returns the static margin to 4 decimals - so differences below 1e-4 vanish and a tolerance of 0 is met only when rounded values coincide, which they did on the default design (converged at a tolerance of 0 in 4 sizings); 20: WARNING, hand-verified by execution, F-104. When the evaluation budget is exhausted (wingPosMaxEval 1) the solver reports 'target SM 15.0% exceeds the maximum attainable' - a statement about the aircraft built from the two or three stations it evaluated, when the target is in fact reached at station 0.343; the base-station design it then returns has a static margin of -0.2667. A negative target is accepted and solved (-0.5) |

The module's units match all six; 4: dn is positive for positive inputs and enters the envelope as 1 + dn on the positive branch and 1 - dn on the negative, which is the symmetric pair 25.341(a)(5)(i) requires for 'positive and negative gusts'; 7: WARNING - the closed form is applied where the regulation requires something else. 25.341(a)(1) requires that loads 'must be determined by dynamic analysis' taking account of unsteady aerodynamics and all significant structural degrees of freedom, and 25.341(a)(3) requires that 'a sufficient number of gust gradient distances in the range 30 feet to 350 feet must be investigated'. This evaluates one algebraic form at one gradient distance. | FORMULA-STRUCT-023 | `src/classes/aircraft/flight-envelope.js:201` | P | W | P | U | U | U | U | U | P | U | U | U | U | U | U | U | W | P | U | U | 3: units resolved and dimensionally consistent; 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 1: the seed and floor of the V_C fixed point. The 1.5 is UNSOURCED and can govern V_C at high wing loading; see F-57; 2: WARNING - the symbol is named a FALLBACK for V_C and the comment at l.193 calls it 'the REGULATORY MINIMUM, not a guess at the real V_C', but the expression is a maximum of that minimum AND an unsourced 1.5 V_S1 floor. The name and the comment describe only one of the two. See F-57; 17: WARNING - genuinely circular, BY REGULATION, and resolved rather than hidden. 25.335(d)(1) makes V_B a function of V_C, and 25.335(a)(2) makes V_C a function of V_B. The module solves the pair with an explicit fixed point at l.202-209 and says so in its comment. The circularity is real and is a property of the rule, not of the implementation; that it is resolved correctly is recorded under question 18; 18: PROVEN STABLE, not assumed. The V_C fixed point at l.202-209 was re-implemented with instrumentation and measured: the iteration map is a CONTRACTION, with abs(f'(V_C*)) = 0.1935 transport, 0.2438 bizjet, 0.2404 turboprop, all well inside the < 1 a contraction requires. Seeded from 0.01 V_S1 to 1000 V_S1 - seven seeds spanning five orders of magnitude - every run converges to the same V_C within 5.7e-7 kt, so the answer does not depend on the initial guess. It takes 12 to 15 iterations against the cap of 40, i.e. 2.7 times headroom, and a hostile case at 5 lb/ft^2 with a 60 ft chord seeded at 1e6 kt still converged in 12. The one gap is reporting, not stability: the loop carries no convergence flag, so exhausting the cap would be indistinguishable from converging. See F-58 |

Seeded from 0.01 V_S1 to 1000 V_S1 - seven seeds spanning five orders of magnitude - every run converges to the same V_C within 5.7e-7 kt, so the answer does not depend on the initial guess. It takes 12 to 15 iterations against the cap of 40, i.e. 2.7 times headroom, and a hostile case at 5 lb/ft^2 with a 60 ft chord seeded at 1e6 kt still converged in 12. The reference speed is therefore a regulatory fallback, not a design value, and q_NE inherits that |
| FORMULA-STRUCT-124 | `src/classes/aircraft/fuselage-structure.js:119` | P | P | U | P | U | U | P | U | P | U | U | U | U | U | U | U | P | U | P | W | 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 1: Ramanujan's second approximation for the perimeter of an ellipse, P = pi (a+b) (1 + 3h/(10 + sqrt(4-3h))) with h = ((a-b)/(a+b))^2. The coded form matches an independently written Ramanujan II to 4.4e-16 over 20,000 (a,b) pairs, differing only in the last bit because it forms (a-b)^2/(a+b)^2 rather than squaring the ratio. Verified against the EXACT perimeter computed two independent ways -- the complete elliptic integral E by the arithmetic-geometric mean, and 240-node Gauss-Legendre quadrature of 4a times the integral of sqrt(1 - m sin^2 t) -- which agree with each other to 4.4e-15. Relative error 0.0e+0 at a=b, 1.8e-15 at 1.05:1, 2.5e-12 at 1.5:1, 4.6e-10 at 2:1, 3.3e-8 at 3:1; 2: a and b are the two semi-axes and h is Ramanujan's lambda^2 with lambda = (a-b)/(a+b). Both roles confirmed by behaviour: h = 0 at a = b and the expression then collapses to 2 pi a, i.e. pi d, exactly; 4: the perimeter is positive for positive semi-axes -- h is a ratio of squares so h >= 0, the bracket is therefore at least 1, and P >= pi (a+b) > 0; 7: applied well inside its validity range. Ramanujan II is valid for all 0 <= b <= a and degrades only with eccentricity; the declared input population reaches an axis ratio of at most 3.125:1 (transport H 25 ft against W 8 ft), where the measured relative error is 3.3e-8. It reaches 1.2e-5 only at 10:1 and 2.4e-4 at 100:1, neither of which any input can produce; 17: not circular. The whole module is a single forward pass with no fixed point anywhere: ellipsePerimeter is a pure function of its two arguments, the perimeter and the equal-area diameter and the vent area all descend from W and H, the two bulkhead stations from L and the two cone lengths, and the frame pitch from the shell length and the frozen class pitch. Every arrow runs from inputs to outputs and no relation appears in its own dependency cone; 19: exactly homogeneous of degree 1, as a perimeter must be -- scaling both semi-axes by k scales the result by k, measured 2.000000000000 at k=2 and 10.000000000000 at k=10; 20: WARNING -- hand-verified rather than scanned. three unguarded holes, all in the exported function and none reachable from the module's own call site. ellipsePerimeter(0, 0) returns NaN because h is 0/0. ellipsePerimeter(-1, -1) returns -6.283185, a NEGATIVE perimeter, silently. Any inverted axis with b/a < 0 large enough to put h above 4/3 makes sqrt(4 - 3h) imaginary, so ellipsePerimeter(1, -3) with h = 4 and ellipsePerimeter(1, -0.5) with h = 9 both return NaN. The one in-module call passes W/2 and H/2 |
| FORMULA-STRUCT-126 | `src/classes/aircraft/fuselage-structure.js:126` | P | P | P | P | P | U | P | U | P | P | P | U | U | U | U | U | P | U | P | W | 3: units resolved and dimensionally consistent; 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 10: source DIRECTLY STATED; 1: 14 CFR 25.365(e)(2) verbatim in the vendored extract classes/transport/extracted/14CFR_25.365.txt l.17 -- 'P = (A s /6240) + .024' -- with H_o = P A_s at l.15 and 'need not exceed 20 square feet' at l.16. All three reproduced longhand over the 1,029 pressurized cases of the declared input population: worst absolute difference in P 0.0e+0 and in H_o 0.0e+0; 2: the regulation defines every symbol in the extract itself -- 'A s = Maximum cross-sectional area of the pressurized shell normal to the longitudinal axis, in square feet' and 'H o = Maximum opening in square feet'. The module divides the metric area by M2_PER_FT2 first, so A_s is in the rule's own unit, and the value it passes IS the maximum: both fuselage outlines carry a straight segment at constant plus or minus W/2 and plus or minus H/2 running from x = noseLength to x = L - tailLength, which is exactly the shell extent, so the section is constant over the shell and its constant value is therefore its maximum; 4: P and H_o are positive for positive A_s, and the 20 ft^2 limit is applied as an UPPER bound, which is the direction the rule states with 'need not exceed'; 5: the reference condition is the regulation's own -- the MAXIMUM section of the PRESSURIZED shell, normal to the longitudinal axis, in square feet. The shell extent used is [noseLength, L - tailLength], which is exactly the constant-section barrel between the two cones, so the reference surface and the measured area coincide; 7: 25.365 opens 'For airplanes with one or more pressurized compartments', and the module applies it to exactly the three Part 25 classes in its frozen PRESSURIZED set. The trainer is refused outright, returning applies false with the words 'Not evaluated is not a pass', confirmed by execution on all 343 trainer cases. The 20 ft^2 cap is genuinely reachable rather than decorative: it binds in 63 of the 1,029 pressurized cases, onset at A_s = 286.24 ft^2, which is an equivalent diameter of 19.09 ft against the module's own stated 'about a 20 ft equivalent diameter'; 11: A_s is used once in P and once in H_o = P A_s. That makes it quadratic, which is the regulation's own form, not a second application of the same quantity; 17: not circular. The whole module is a single forward pass with no fixed point anywhere: ellipsePerimeter is a pure function of its two arguments, the perimeter and the equal-area diameter and the vent area all descend from W and H, the two bulkhead stations from L and the two cone lengths, and the frame pitch from the shell length and the frozen class pitch. Every arrow runs from inputs to outputs and no relation appears in its own dependency cone; 19: deliberately NOT scale-free, and correctly so. P carries the regulation's constant term, so H_o = A_s^2/6240 + 0.024 A_s grows faster than the area: A_s of 50, 100, 200 and 400 ft^2 give H_o of 1.601, 4.003, 11.210 and 35.241 ft^2. The non-homogeneity is the rule's own and the 20 ft^2 cap bounds it from above; 20: WARNING -- hand-verified rather than scanned. unguarded on its argument. decompressionVent(-1 ft^2) returns H_o = -0.2397 ft^2, a negative required opening, with capped false. decompressionVent(Infinity) returns P Infinity and H_o 20 with capped TRUE, which is a clean, plausible, fully-capped answer produced from an infinite input. decompressionVent(NaN) returns NaN with capped false. The one in-module call passes a positive crossSectionM2 |
| FORMULA-STRUCT-129 | `src/classes/aircraft/fuselage-structure.js:151` | P | P | U | P | U | U | P | U | P | U | P | U | U | U | U | U | P | U | P | W | 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 1: the perimeter of the drawn section, taking the semi-axes W/2 and H/2 of an ellipse of width W and height H. Reproduces ellipsePerimeter(W/2, H/2) longhand over all 1,372 cases at rel 0.0e+0. Ramanujan II is symmetric in its arguments, verified, so no major/minor ordering is required of the caller; 2: W and H are the sized fuselage width and height, so W/2 and H/2 are the semi-axes the callee expects; 4: a positive length for a positive section; 7: inherits the validity of STRUCT-124 and is called only with the semi-axes of a sized section; measured axis ratios across the declared population stay at or below 3.125:1; 11: the perimeter of one section is computed TWICE in this tool by two different methods, and this is the one place it could hide a double count. It does not. Ramanujan II here sets the REPORTED stringer pitch; a 4000-step inscribed polygon in geometry.js sectionStringerPoints sets the DRAWN spacing, and that module states the split itself -- 'the two are not the same number and neither depends on the other'. They are two estimates of one quantity used for two purposes, each applied once. Measured over all 1,372 cases the worst relative gap between them is 2.6e-8, and an inscribed polygon under-reads, so that is a floor; 17: not circular. The whole module is a single forward pass with no fixed point anywhere: ellipsePerimeter is a pure function of its two arguments, the perimeter and the equal-area diameter and the vent area all descend from W and H, the two bulkhead stations from L and the two cone lengths, and the frame pitch from the shell length and the frozen class pitch. Applying the bias growth (32)-(33) to a radial whose table values are already grown would count the growth twice. Measured on the 27 radial rows, a second growth would add 0.198 to 0.402 in of bay half-height, 1.95 to 3.03 per cent of D_G. The module declines it, and the primary supports declining it: the table's Grown Max columns are what the database carries. The bias path applies G_W and G_H exactly once each, and clearances reads W_G from the envelope rather than regrowing it, so the two cannot drift apart; 17: not circular - G7 is terminal. stowage is written into gear.js's return at l.166 and read by no sizing path; the only other mentions of it in the tree are comments. The tyre it rests on is selected from the load, which descends from MTOW, so the dependency runs one way and nothing stowage computes re-enters the loop that produced its inputs. It returns no mass and no energy; 19: homogeneous of degree 1 in length, so a consistent rescale cannot degrade it - verified by rescaling the whole tyre row by 0.0254 and recovering D_G to rel 0.0e+0. AR and the growth factors are dimensionless; 20: WARNING - hand-verified, not scanned. The guard at l.64 requires D, W and D_O finite and positive but never requires D_O > D, so an inverted row is accepted: forcing odMaxIn 5 against rimDiaIn 12 returns resolved true with D_G 5 and H -3.5, and on the bias path H = -1 with D_G 2.909, neither refused. See F-56 |

(24), the report's single continuous force form, as 2.4 times the bracket x - Cz(1 - exp(-0.6x/Cz)) with x the deflection ratio delta/w. Reproduced against an independently written transcription at abs difference 0.0e+0 over 4,000 values of x in (0, 4], and the large-x asymptote 2.4(x - Cz) is exact at x = 5. Strictly increasing and positive throughout, measured; 2: x is TN 4110's deflection ratio delta/w, deflection over SECTION WIDTH, and Cz is the report's type constant. The report's own non-dimensionalisation of the left side, F_z over (p + 0.08 p_r) w sqrt(w d), is legible in the vendored text and is deliberately absent from the module, because the efficiency is a ratio in which it cancels; 4: the force is positive and strictly increasing on (0, 4], measured at 4,000 points, which is what a tyre carrying load must do; 7: applied well inside its range. The bracket is smooth and monotone for every positive x, and the deflection ratios the shipped tyre table can produce span 0.018750 to 0.378378 across all 294 rows, measured; 8: WARNING -- an empirical constant is carried one tyre type beyond the data it was measured on, declared but not avoided. Cz is empirical: the vendored TN 4110 text states twice that Cz = 0.02 for type I tires and 0.03 for types III and VII. The module applies the type III and VII value to EVERY row, including the 27 radial tyres in the table, and says so in a radialNote recording that radials post-date the 1957 report and that it offers no radial figure. That is honest about the extrapolation and better than inventing a radial constant, but it is still an empirical constant applied outside its measured population, and nothing bounds the error it introduces; 17: not circular. The module is a single forward pass with no fixed point: the force function depends only on its two arguments, the efficiency on the deflection ratio and the type constant, the deflection ratio on three tabulated lengths, and the stroke on inputs all fixed before it is called. No relation appears in its own dependency cone, and the caller supplies gravity rather than the module reaching back for it; 19: dimensionless in a dimensionless argument, so scale-free by construction: scaling a tyre's deflection and section width together leaves x unchanged and therefore the force function unchanged; 20: WARNING -- hand-verified, and the previous note on this row was the analyser rather than the code; see F-65. The enclosing function DOES guard, at l.92, and the scan could not see it. Three holes remain, hand-verified: the guard returns 0 rather than refusing, so a NaN deflection yields a FORCE OF ZERO, which is a plausible number a consumer will use; the Cz argument is unguarded, so Cz = 0 silently returns the un-offset line and a negative Cz returns a negative force, measured -10.0137 at x = 0.25; and x = Infinity returns Infinity. Latent: the only in-module call passes a positive ratio and the frozen constant |

TN 4110 carries both, listing a static and a dynamic force-deflection variation and a whole section of hysteresis properties, and LANDING-GEAR.md records its vertical hysteresis parameter at about 0.1 while saying it is *not the ratio of absorbed energy to L_max delta*. The static route is not an invention: it is exactly option (b) of that note's own gap 1, *integrate the TN 4110 static force-deflection curve to derive eta_t honestly*. Reproduced longhand on all 294 tabulated tyres at abs difference 0.0e+0; 2: WARNING -- the symbols are right but the diameter is not the one the cited relation is written on. The deflection is formed from odMaxIn, the MAXIMUM tabulated outside diameter, while the data book's own static-loaded-radius relation, LANDING-GEAR.md eq. (10), is written on D_M, the MEAN overall tyre diameter: SLR = D_M/2 - d (D_M - D_F)/2. Using the maximum overstates the deflection ratio by a mean of 6.98 per cent across the 267 rows that carry a minimum diameter, and on one row it is the difference between a resolvable and an unresolvable tyre, since the mean puts the deflection negative there. This output is used as a MOMENT in N m (it is the moment part of the wrench the allocator turns into rotor thrusts). The gains are ArduPilot's EDU450 rate gains, whose units are s/rad and whose output is a NORMALISED mixer command: AC_AttitudeControl_Multi passes update_all(...) to _motors.set_roll(), and AP_MotorsMatrix scales roll factors to +-0.5. One unit of command is therefore about 0.5 fMax sum abs(y_i) N m - about 2.55 N m on a 450-class quad (0.225 m arm, 8 N motors), 21.2 N m at 1 m with 30 N motors, 127 N m for a 1.5 m heavy-lift with 120 N motors - and the module's rate loop is that many times softer than ArduPilot's. A zero, negative or NaN MTOW gives a DL of 0 or less and the dynamics refuse it (applicable false), but a NaN MTOW is reported as a disk loading of 0 rather than unknown. A rotor body without a radius contributes zero area through r.radius OR 0 and is still counted as a rotor; a zero total area gives DL 0, refused downstream |
| FORMULA-STAB-173 | `src/engine/whirlflutter.js:122` | P | W | P | P | W | U | W | P | P | U | U | U | U | U | U | U | P | U | P | W | 1: the reference boundary over the cruise speed converted to knots, recomputed independently with the knot taken from its definition, 3600/1852: 3,519 cases over t/c 0.08 to 0.30 and 5 to 200 m/s, 0 disagreements beyond the two-decimal rounding of the output; the reference is 335 kt at t/c >= 0.23 and 275 kt otherwise on every case. KT_PER_MS 1.943844 differs from 3600/1852 by -2.5e-7; 2: WARNING, F-92. The ratio is defined against CRUISE speed. 14 CFR 23.2245(a) requires freedom from flutter at all speeds within and sufficiently beyond the structural design envelope, so the speed a flutter margin is shown against is the top of that envelope with a margin, not cruise, and a ratio to cruise overstates the margin by the dive-to-cruise ratio. The verdict that reads the ratio also misnames it: for a cruise speed from 211.54 to 274.99 kt, below the 275 kt boundary, it prints 'cruise is AT OR ABOVE the reference boundary', measured; 3: units resolved and dimensionally consistent; 4: a ratio of two positive speeds. A zero or negative cruise speed is refused at the site (vKt > 0) and returns no ratio, measured at 0 and -10 m/s; 5: WARNING, from the primary. Acree's 335 and 275 kt are true airspeeds at sea-level standard conditions, rotor at 457 rpm (76 % of hover design rpm), limited-power trim (Acree 1999, p.2 and Appendix). None of these conditions is carried into the result, and the caller's cruise speed is compared with the sea-level boundary whatever the cruise altitude or rotor speed; 7: WARNING, measured. The reference is one airframe, the XV-15, applied to every tiltrotor, hybrid and side-by-side layout, which the module states. Within the published pair the choice is conservative: a wing of t/c 0.20 to 0.229, nearer the thick wing, receives the thin wing's 275 kt. Below the thin wing it is not: thinnerThanReference is set but the verdict never reads it, so t/c 0.08 at 60 m/s reads 'cruise sits far below the reference boundary' at a ratio of 2.36 against a boundary the module itself calls 'at least this bad'. F-92; 8: no relation is fitted. Every t/c from 0.08 to 0.30 maps to exactly one of the two published boundaries and none is interpolated or extrapolated numerically, measured; the shortfall below t/c 0.15 is recorded under 7; 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 17: a pure function of its three inputs and two published constants; 19: inversely proportional to the cruise speed and independent of every other input, measured; the unit conversion is exact to 2.5e-7; 20: WARNING, hand-verified by execution. The site's own test refuses a zero, negative, NaN, infinite, null, empty or absent speed and reports 'cruise speed unknown', which conflates an invalid speed with an unknown one, and a negative speed still prints vCruiseKt -19.4. A NaN or absent t/c silently takes the thin-wing boundary and reports t/c null; a null t/c becomes 0 and sets thinnerThanReference true; the string '70' is coerced to 70 m/s; 1e-9 m/s gives a ratio of 1.4e11. No production code calls the function (F-92) |

Unguarded: a zero weight returns Infinity and nothing refuses a negative wing loading, which returns NaN. LATENT behind validated inputs. See F-82 |
| FORMULA-MISSION-006 | `src/classes/trainer/performance.js:92` | P | P | U | U | P | P | U | U | P | P | U | U | U | U | U | U | P | U | P | W | 1: the Breguet form R = 375 (eta/c)(L/D) ln(W1/W2) with R in STATUTE miles, converted to nautical by NM_PER_SM. The 375 is not taken on trust: it is exactly 550 ft.lb/s per horsepower x 3600 s/h / 5280 ft per statute mile = 375, verified to be exactly 375 in floating point. The relation round-trips against the fuel relation at 6.8e-15 over 108 combinations; 2: eta is propeller efficiency, sfc the specific fuel consumption in lb/hp/h, LD the lift-drag ratio and the weights are gross and gross-minus-fuel; 5: the primary's range is in STATUTE miles and the module converts with NM_PER_SM, which matches the exact 1609.344/1852 to 4.2e-8; 6: the efficiency enters in the direction that improves performance, MEASURED: raising eta from 0.70 to 0.85 raises the range from 1109.03 to 1346.68 nm, and range divided by eta is invariant at 0.0e+0, so the dependence is exactly linear; 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 10: source DIRECTLY STATED; 17: a single forward pass; 19: measured homogeneous of degree +1 in L/D and -1 in sfc, both EXACT, and the range is INVARIANT at 0.0e+0 when fuel and start weight are doubled together - correct, since it depends only on the weight ratio; 20: WARNING, hand-verified. Unguarded at the physical boundary: fuel equal to the start weight returns Infinity and fuel GREATER than the start weight returns NaN, from the logarithm of a non-positive ratio. LATENT behind validated inputs. See F-82 |

| FORMULA-REG-001 | `src/classes/aircraft/compliance.js:118` | P | P | U | P | F | U | W | U | P | U | P | U | U | U | U | U | P | U | P | W | 1: the inversion of Scholz (5.14), T/W = nE/(nE-1) (1/E + gamma), for gamma at the design T/W, recomputed from the matching chart's OWN definition of each line (transport/size.js:200,203) rather than from this shortcut. The second segment reproduces at relative 2.2e-16 or better on nine configurations - three engine counts by three fuel methods - and on the bizjet, and back-solving the gradient at the chart's own twReq returns the regulation's 0.024 to 6.9e-18. The expression is right; where it is applied is not - see 5; 2: req is the 25.121 minimum, read verbatim from the vendored 14 CFR 25.121 text - 2.4/2.7/3.0 % at (b)(1) and 2.1/2.4/2.7 % at (d)(1), the same tables as transport/size.js:51-52; twDesign is all-engine sea-level static thrust over MTOW weight, which is Scholz's T_TO/(m_MTO g); twReq is the chart's line; nE is clamped to 2-4, the only engine counts 25.121 tabulates, and agrees with the chart's unclamped nE on every live configuration; 4: positive margin means compliant, uniformly, and achieved - required equals the margin exactly; 5: FAIL, hand-verified against the primary. Scholz (5.22)-(5.24), vendored, states the missed-approach line at the maximum LANDING mass and converts it to take-off mass by m_ML/m_MTO, which transport/size.js:203 does. Inverting that line for gamma therefore needs the T/W divided by the same ratio, and this relation omits it: the B-17 margin it reports is lambda times the true one, exactly. Measured 16.00 % low on the transport (lambda 0.84) at every engine count and fuel method, 10.50 % low on the bizjet (0.895); achieved 7.79 % against a true 8.87 %, and 16.66 % against 18.36 %. The verdict cannot flip, because lambda > 0 preserves the sign of the surplus, so it is conservative and LOW. The module separately DECLARES its static-thrust basis as optimistic; that is not this defect. F-84; 7: WARNING, measured. Scholz (5.17) substitutes the climb gradient for sin(gamma) because 'the angle is small'; true at the requirement (0.03 % at 2.4 %) and no longer at the achieved values this row reports - the bizjet's 17.33 % second-segment figure is sin(gamma), whose tangent, the regulation's gradient, is 17.60 %, 1.54 % apart; 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 11: the one-engine-inoperative factor is applied exactly ONCE. twReq already carries nE/(nE-1), the inversion applies (nE-1)/nE once to the surplus, and the back-solve at twReq lands on the requirement to 7e-18; 17: not circular. The design T/W is itself the maximum over the chart's lines (transport/size.js:424), but this relation reads the converged result after sizing and feeds nothing back; 19: affine in the design T/W with slope exactly (nE-1)/nE, measured at relative 0.0e+0 for two, three and four engines on two successive steps; 20: WARNING, hand-verified by execution. l.114 refuses a non-finite T/W on either side and the row becomes NOT EVALUATED, measured for NaN and Infinity. Not covered: a non-integer or NaN engine count reaches GRAD[nE] as undefined and the row reports FAIL with a NaN achieved value and NaN margin - measured on a synthetic result, and unreachable from the engine, measured: sizing the transport at 2.5 engines gives a NaN twSecond from the chart, which l.114 refuses first. Zero and negative T/W give a FAIL, which is the correct answer. The turboprop never reaches l.118 at all: its chart reports the same two lines as power-to-mass (pmSecond, pmMissed) and the guard reads only twSecond, so both rows are NOT EVALUATED under the false reason that the chart does not report them. F-84 |
| FORMULA-REG-002 | `src/classes/aircraft/compliance.js:144` | P | P | P | P | P | U | U | U | P | U | U | U | U | U | U | U | P | U | P | W | 1: newtons to kilograms by standard gravity, reproduced against the mission's own pound figure - trip/9.80665 = 15,788.2234 kg on the transport and tripLb x 0.45359237 = 15,788.2234 kg, relative 2.2e-16; the bizjet 2,981.550 kg at 0.0e+0; 2: trip is flyMission's TRIP fuel weight (transport/mission.js:242), taxi + take-off + climb + cruise + descent and NO reserve, carried in newtons because the mission is flown in newtons from a ramp weight in newtons (transport/size.js:313); the row's note says reserves are still aboard at landing, which is what 25.25(a) and this definition both mean; 3: units resolved and dimensionally consistent; 4: the trip fuel is positive and is subtracted from the take-off mass; 5: the reference conditions are the mission's own. The G divided here, 9.80665, is the G the mission multiplied by (transport/size.js:50, transport/mission.js:48), and the take-off mass is the ramp weight the mission was flown from. The resulting landing mass matches the mission's own landingWeightN/G to 0.196 kg in 63,012, 3.1e-6; 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 17: a conversion of a converged output, not circular; 19: exactly linear, doubling the trip doubles the mass; 20: WARNING, hand-verified by execution. A zero or negative trip is not guarded and reports a landing mass at or above MTOW and a FAIL, measured on a synthetic result and unreachable from the mission |
| FORMULA-REG-009 | `src/engine/certification.js:145` | P | W | P | U | U | U | W | U | P | U | U | U | U | U | U | U | P | P | P | W | 1: the upper end of the payload bisection bracket, hi = max(1, maxPayloadKg if given, else twice the design payload, else twice 455 kg), recomputed longhand for ten inputs and reproduced on every one - 455 and an absent or null payload give 910, a 300 kg payload gives 600, zero or negative payloads and bounds floor at 1; 2: WARNING. hi, lo and best are what they claim to be, but n is not: the returned note prints n as the number of sizing evaluations while n counts only the bisection steps and omits the bound probe at hi, measured with a counting wrapper as 10 reported against 11 made at the 3,175 kg limit. The sibling maxRangeAtMTOM starts its count at 2 for exactly that reason. The lower bracket lo = 0 is assumed feasible and is never evaluated - see 7 and F-83; 3: units resolved and dimensionally consistent; 7: WARNING, measured. The bracket's lower end is OUTSIDE the sizing loop's own input domain - a zero payload throws inside a cabin display string (already F-R16), which feasible() catches as infeasible - and it is never tested. When nothing in the bracket closes the function returns that untested end as its answer: at a 500 kg limit, where even a 1 kg payload sizes to an MTOW of 771.2 kg, it reports payloadKg 0 under the note 'bisection on payload, 10 sizing evaluations', while maxRangeAtMTOM asked the same question reports 'no mission closes inside the limit - the airframe alone exceeds it'. At a range of 400 km it does the same at the default limit. F-83; 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 17: not circular. The sizing function is injected rather than imported precisely so this module has no dependency on engine.js, and the bracket is fixed before the first evaluation; 18: stable, measured. Bisection halves the interval every step and terminates on the 1 kg tolerance or the iteration cap max(6, 22): the 910 kg bracket closes in exactly ceil(log2 910) = 10 steps, as observed. Its premise also holds - MTOW rises monotonically in payload, 0 decreases over 91 sized points from 10 to 910 kg at a mean 4.31 kg of MTOW per kg of payload - so feasibility is a single step and the bisection brackets it; 19: linear in the design payload with a factor of exactly 2 and a floor of 1, measured on 455, 300 and the absent case; 20: WARNING, hand-verified by execution. The l.136 finite-check on the LIMIT stands as recorded in the Q20 integrity block, and nothing guards the bracket itself: a NaN payload or a NaN maxPayloadKg makes hi NaN, because Math.max(1, NaN) is NaN, the loop test (NaN - 0) > 1 is false, and the function returns payloadKg 0 with the note 'bisection on payload, 0 sizing evaluations' after making one. A NaN limitKg fails the l.136 check and is reported as 'no limit set', which conflates an invalid limit with a deliberate absence of one. No caller in src or validation reaches either: both inverse functions are exported from engine.js and consumed nowhere |

F-87; 2: WARNING. Described as a unit conversion carrying lbf to N, lbf/ft2 to N/m2 and ft2 to m2. A constant expression with no runtime input, so there is nothing to guard and nothing guards it |

| FORMULA-REG-032 | `src/engine/noise.js:152` | F | U | U | U | W | U | U | U | P | P | U | U | U | U | U | U | P | U | P | W | 1: FAIL, hand-verified against the primary. Labelled the bare Gutin loading pressure. Gutin's thrust term, from Marte and Kurtz equation (1), is m B Omega T cos(theta) / (2 sqrt2 pi c r) and has units of pressure. The coded B Omega T / (4 pi r rho c^2 sqrt2) divides by rho c^2 where the primary divides by c, so it has units of m/s, not pascals, and sits 58.4 dB below the primary's prefactor at sea level. The calibration constant then restores about 12 to 15 dB. Net, the shipped fundamental is 0.7 dB below Gutin's maximum on a five-blade rotor and 14.4 dB below it on the three-blade default. The register records this row's source as DIRECTLY STATED, and Q10 is left as the register has it. F-88; 5: WARNING.
